0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\cdots.

As in (
2.50
), we then define specialization maps with respect to the choice of the uniformizer
\pi\in R
by the formula

(2.53)

sp^{\pi}:H^{i}(F_{c}X_{\eta},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{i}(F_{c}X_{0},\mathbb{Z}/n(j)),\ \alpha\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces-\partial(\alpha\cup(\pi)),

where the cup product
\cup(\pi)
is given by (
2.51
) and where the residue map
\partial
is simply the co-boundary map of (
2.52
).

We have the following list of properties.

Proposition 2.13
.

(
[
Sch24a
, Proposition 4.1]
)
With notations as fixed at the beginning of
\lx@sectionsign
2.6
, the specialization map

sp^{\pi}:H^{i}(F_{c}X_{\eta},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{i}(F_{c}X_{0},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))

from (
2.53
) satisfies the following properties:

(1)

sp^{\pi}
does not depend on the uniformizer
\pi
if
k
is algebraically closed,

(2)

sp^{\pi}
respects the filtration
F_{\ast}
,

(3)

For any class
[\alpha]\in H^{p}_{c,nr}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}/n(m))
and any class
\beta\in H^{i}(F_{c}X_{\eta},\mathbb{Z}/n(j)),
we have that
sp^{\pi}(\alpha|_{F_{c}X_{\eta}}\cup\beta)=\alpha|_{F_{c}X_{0}}\cup sp^{\pi}(\beta)
. In particular,
sp^{\pi}(\alpha|_{F_{c}X_{\eta}})=\alpha|_{F_{c}X_{0}}
.

(4)

The specialization map
sp^{\pi}
commutes with the pushforward maps given in (
2.13
), i.e., if
\mathcal{Y}\to\operatorname{Spec}R
is another smooth family with equi-dimensional fibres and
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
is a proper
R
-morphism of pure relative dimension, then
(f|_{X_{0}})_{\ast}\circ sp^{\pi}=sp^{\pi}\circ(f|_{X_{\eta}})_{\ast}
.

(5)

The map
sp^{\pi}
induces a specialization map between the geometric fibres
X_{\bar{\eta}}=X_{\eta}\times\bar{K}
and
X_{\bar{0}}=X_{0}\times_{k}\bar{k}
:

\bar{sp}:H^{i}(F_{c}X_{\bar{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{i}(F_{c}X_{\bar{0}},\mathbb{Z}/n(j)).

In addition, this respects the filtration
F_{\ast}
and does not depend on the choice of uniformizer
\pi\in R
.

Proof.

For the proof see
[
Sch24a
, Proposition 4.1]
. We only explain (
3
). Since
\alpha\in H^{p}(F_{c+1}\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}/n(m)),
we may find an open subset
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathcal{X}
such that
F_{c+1}\mathcal{X}\subset\mathcal{U}
and such that
\alpha\in H^{p}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{Z}/n(m))
. In particular, we have that
F_{c}X_{0}\subset\mathcal{U}
. Now let
\beta\in H^{i}(F_{c}X_{\eta},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))
and pick a closed subset
W\subset X_{\eta}
such that
\dim X_{\eta}-\dim W\geq c+1
and such that
\beta\in H^{i}(U_{\eta}\setminus W,\mathbb{Z}/n(j))
, where
U_{\eta}=\mathcal{U}\times_{k}K
(and here note that we explicitly used that
F_{c}U_{\eta}=F_{c}X_{\eta}
). We set
\mathcal{W}\subset\mathcal{U}
for the closure of
W
inside
\mathcal{U}
and denote by
W_{0}:=\mathcal{W}\times_{R}k
and
W_{\eta}=\mathcal{W}\times_{R}K
its central and generic fibre, respectively. We then simply apply Lemma
2.11
to the smooth family
\mathcal{U}\setminus\mathcal{W}\to\operatorname{Spec}R
and classes
\alpha|_{\mathcal{U}\setminus\mathcal{W}}\in H^{p}(\mathcal{U}\setminus\mathcal{W},\mathbb{Z}/n(m))
and
\beta\in H^{i}(U_{\eta}\setminus W_{\eta},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))
. That is, we get
sp^{\pi}(\alpha|_{U_{\eta}\setminus W_{\eta}}\cup\beta)=\alpha|_{U_{0}\setminus W_{0}}\cup sp^{\pi}(\beta)
. This in turn implies what we want as
F_{c}X_{\eta}\subset U_{\eta}\setminus W_{\eta}
and
F_{c}X_{0}\subset U_{0}\setminus W_{0}
. The second claim of (
3
) follows from the first; cf. proof of Lemma
2.11
.
∎

Remark 2.14
.

By (
2
) and (
5
) one obtains well-defined specialization maps

sp^{\pi}:H^{i}_{c,nr}(X_{\eta},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{i}_{c,nr}(X_{0},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))\ \text{and}\ \bar{sp}:H^{i}_{c,nr}(X_{\bar{\eta}},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{i}_{c,nr}(X_{\bar{0}},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))

for refined unramified cohomology; see
[
Sch24a
, Corollary 4.2]
. The properties (
1
)–(
5
) can also be formulated accordingly for these maps. In particular,
\bar{sp}
does not depend on the choice of uniformizer
\pi\in R
.

3.
Pairings

In this section inspired by
[
Sch24b
,
\lx@sectionsign 4
]
, we construct actions of higher Chow cycles on refined unramified cohomology.
We are interested to study the injectivity property of the following pairing.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
k
be a perfect field and let
H^{\ast}(-,A(j))
be any of the cohomology functors (
2.1
)–(
2.5
). Then for any pair of smooth and equi-dimensional algebraic
k
-schemes
X
and
Y
and integers
c,p,i,r\geq 0,
there is a natural pairing

(3.1)

\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p\ ;A)\otimes H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y,A(j))\overset{\times}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}H^{i+2c-p}_{r+c-p,nr}(X\times Y,A(c+j))

which is compatible with the pairing

\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p\ ;A)\otimes H^{i}(Y,A(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{i+2c-p}(X\times Y,A(c+j)),\ [z]\otimes\alpha\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\operatorname{cl}^{2c-p,c}_{X\times Y}(p^{\ast}[z])\cup q^{\ast}\alpha,

where
p:X\times_{k}Y\to X
and
q:X\times_{k}Y\to Y
are the canonical projection maps. That is, the diagram below

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p\ ;A)\otimes H^{i}(Y,A(j))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{i+2c-p}(X\times Y,A(j+c))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p\ ;A)\otimes H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y,A(j))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{i+2c-p}_{r+c-p,nr}(X\times Y,A(j+c))}}

commutes.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{Z}^{c}(X,\bullet)\otimes A
be Bloch’s cycle complex of abelian groups whose homology groups compute higher Chow groups; see
\lx@sectionsign
2.2
. Let
d_{\ast}:\mathcal{Z}^{c}(X,\ast)\otimes A\to\mathcal{Z}^{c}(X,\ast-1)\otimes A
be its differential. We distinguish the cases
c>p
and
c\leq p
. We first treat the case
c\leq p
. Then the compatibility of the cup product with respect to restriction on an open subset yields a well defined exterior product map

\Lambda:H^{2c-p}(X,A(c))\otimes H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y,A(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{2c-p+i}_{r,nr}(X\times Y,A(c+j)),\alpha\otimes[\beta]\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces[p^{\ast}\alpha\cup q^{\ast}\beta].

Since we assumed
c-p\leq 0
, we find a natural restriction map

\operatorname{res}:H^{2c-p+i}_{r,nr}(X\times Y,A(c+j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{2c-p+i}_{r+c-p,nr}(X\times Y,A(c+j))

and thus, the pairing (
3.1
) can be then defined as the composite map
\operatorname{res}\circ\Lambda\circ(\operatorname{cl}_{X}\otimes\operatorname{id}),
where
\operatorname{cl}_{X}:\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p\ ;A)\to H^{2c-p}(X,A(c))
is the corresponding cycle class map.

Next, we consider the case
c>p
and construct a natural pairing

(3.2)

\ker(d_{p})\otimes H^{i}(F_{r}Y,A(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{i+2c-p}(F_{r+c-p}(X\times Y),A(j+c)).

Pick a cycle
\Gamma\in\ker(d_{p})
and set
|\Gamma|:=\operatorname{supp}(\Gamma)\subset X\times\Delta^{p}
for its support. We consider the projection map
\operatorname{pr}_{1}:X\times\Delta^{p}\to X
and note that
Z:=\overline{\operatorname{pr}_{1}(|\Gamma|)}\subset X
has codimension at least
c-p
in
X
. Up to enlarging this closed subscheme, we may assume that
Z\subset X
is equi-dimensional of codimension-
(c-p)
in
X
and that
[\Gamma]\in\operatorname{CH}^{p}(Z,p\ ;A)
. There is a canonical homomorphism
\rho:H^{2c-p}_{Z}(X_{\text{Zar}},A_{X}(c)_{\text{Zar}})\to H^{2c-p}_{Z}(X,A(c)),
where
H^{\ast}_{Z}(X,A(c))
is the appropriate cohomology group with support; see (
2.6
). We thus obtain a cycle class map

(3.3)

\operatorname{cl}_{Z}:\operatorname{CH}^{p}(Z,p\ ;A)\simeq H^{2c-p}_{Z}(X_{\text{Zar}},A_{X}(c)_{\text{Zar}})\overset{\rho}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}H^{2c-p}_{Z}(X,A(c))

and consider the class
\operatorname{cl}_{Z}(\Gamma)\in H^{2c-p}_{Z}(X,A(c))
.

On the other hand, let
\alpha\in H^{i}(F_{r}Y,A(j))
and choose a lift
\tilde{\alpha}\in H^{i}(U,A(j))
over some open subset
U\subset X
whose complement
R:=Y\setminus U
has codimension at least
r+1
in
Y
. We consider the following composite

(3.4)

\begin{split}&H^{i}(U,A(j))\xrightarrow{\text{Exc}\circ\operatorname{cl}_{Z\times U}(p^{\ast}\Gamma)\cup q^{\ast}}H^{i+2c-p}_{Z\times U}(X\times Y\setminus(Z\times R),A(c+j))\\
&\overset{\iota_{\ast}}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}H^{i+2c-p}(X\times Y\setminus(Z\times R),A(c+j)),\end{split}

where

\cup:H^{2c-p}_{Z\times U}(X\times U,A(c))\otimes H^{i}(X\times U,A(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{i+2c-p}_{Z\times U}(X\times U,A(c+j))

is the natural cup product pairing for
(H^{\ast},H^{\ast}_{Z})
and where for the middle term of (
3.4
) we use the excision isomorphism
Exc

\ H^{i+2c-p}_{Z\times U}(X\times Y\setminus(Z\times R),A(c+j))\simeq H^{i+2c-p}_{Z\times U}(X\times U,A(c+j)).

Now the closed subset
Z\times R\subset X\times Y
has codimension at least
c-p+r+1
in
X\times Y
and thus we can define
\Gamma\times\alpha:=[\iota_{\ast}\text{Exc}(\operatorname{cl}_{Z\times U}(p^{\ast}\Gamma)\cup\tilde{\alpha})]\in H^{i+2c-p}(F_{c-p+r}(X\times Y),A(c+j))
. Note that the class
\Gamma\times\alpha
is well defined, i.e., it is independent of our choices of a lift
\tilde{\alpha}
and a closed subscheme
Z\subset X
such that
|\Gamma|\subset Z\times\Delta^{p}
. In fact, the former follows as any two lifts of
\alpha
coincide over an open subset that contains
F_{r}Y
. In addition, the covariant functoriality of the cycle map
\operatorname{cl}_{Z}
for proper maps implies the latter, since a diagram of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{i_{1}}_{Z}(X,A(j_{1}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{i_{1}}_{Z^{\prime}}(X,A(j_{1}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{i_{1}+i_{2}}_{Z}(X,A(j_{1}+j_{2}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{i_{1}+i_{2}}_{Z^{\prime}}(X,A(j_{1}+j_{2}))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\ast}}

always commutes for
\beta\in H^{i_{2}}(X,A(j_{2}))
and
Z\subset Z^{\prime}
closed subschemes of
X
(one can see this using the six functor formalism in the resp. derived category for instance).

By construction, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\ker(d_{p})\otimes H^{i}(F_{r+1}Y,A(j))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{2c-p+i}(F_{c-p+r+1}(X\times Y),A(c+j))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\ker(d_{p})\otimes H^{i}(F_{r}Y,A(j))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{2c-p+i}(F_{c-p+r}(X\times Y),A(c+j))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\times}

commutes, thus inducing a pairing

(3.5)

\ker(d_{p})\otimes H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y,A(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{i+2c-p}_{r+c-p,nr}(X\times Y,A(j+c)).

Finally, we show that
\Gamma\times\alpha=0,
if
\Gamma\in\operatorname{im}(d_{p+1})
. In this case, there is an equi-dimensional closed subscheme
W\subset X
of codimension-
(c-p-1)
, such that
|\Gamma|\subset W\times\Delta^{p}
and
\Gamma=0\in\operatorname{CH}^{p+1}(W,p)
. In particular, this implies that its cycle class
\operatorname{cl}_{W}(\Gamma)=0\in H^{2c-p}_{W}(X,A(c))
vanishes. Let
\tilde{\alpha}\in H^{i}(V,A(c))
be a lift of the class
\alpha\in H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y,A(j))
over some open subset
V\subset Y
whose complement
D:=Y\setminus V
has codimension at least
r+2
in
Y
. It can be readily checked that a representative for the class
\Gamma\times\alpha\in H^{i+2c-p}_{r+c-p,nr}(X\times Y,A(j+c))
is given then as the value of
\tilde{\alpha}
by the following map

\begin{split}&H^{i}(V,A(j))\xrightarrow{\text{Exc}\circ\operatorname{cl}_{Z\times U}(p^{\ast}\Gamma)\cup q^{\ast}}H^{i+2c-p}_{W\times V}(X\times Y\setminus(W\times D),A(c+j))\\
&\overset{\iota_{\ast}}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}H^{i+2c-p}(X\times Y\setminus(W\times D),A(c+j)),\end{split}

where as before
Exc
is the excision isomorphism

H^{i+2c-p}_{W\times V}(X\times Y\setminus(W\times D),A(c+j))\simeq H^{i+2c-p}_{W\times V}(X\times V,A(c+j)).

We thus conclude
\Gamma\times\alpha=0,
since
\operatorname{cl}_{W\times V}(p^{\ast}\Gamma)=p^{\ast}\operatorname{cl}_{W}(\Gamma)=0
. The compatibility property for the pairing
\times
is clear by construction. The proof is complete.
∎

Remark 3.2
.

Let
c>p
. In the notation of Lemma
3.1
, if one sets

N^{c-p-1}\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p\ ;A):=\operatorname{im}(\bigoplus_{W\subset X}\ker(\operatorname{cl}_{W})\overset{\iota_{\ast}}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p\ ;A)),

where
W\subset X
runs through equi-dimensional closed subschemes of codimension-
(c-p-1)
in
X
and where the map
\operatorname{cl}_{W}:\operatorname{CH}^{p+1}(W,p\ ;A)\simeq H^{2c-p}_{W}(X_{\text{Zar}},A_{X}(c)_{\text{Zar}})\rightarrow H^{2c-p}_{W}(X,A(c))
is the natural one, then the pairing

N^{c-p-1}\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p\ ;A)\otimes H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y,A(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{i+2c-p}_{r+c-p,nr}(X\times Y,A(c+j))

becomes the zero map. This is implicitly implied by the argument in the last paragraph of the proof in the above lemma.

It also says that in the special case one considers an algebraically closed field
k,
takes
A=\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
with
\ell\neq\operatorname{char}(k)
, sets
p=0
and let
H^{\ast}(-,A(j))
be the cohomology functor given by
\ell
-adic continuous étale cohomology (
2.2
), then (
3.1
) yields a well defined pairing

\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X)/\sim_{\operatorname{alg}}\otimes H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{i+2c}_{r+c,nr}(X\times Y,\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(c+j)),

where
\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X)/\sim_{\operatorname{alg}}
is the Chow group modulo algebraic equivalence; see
[
Sch23
, Lemma 7.5, Proposition 6.6]
.

Next, we compare the natural exterior product maps for higher Chow groups

(3.6)

\operatorname{CH}^{c_{1}}(X,p_{1}\ ;A)\otimes\operatorname{CH}^{c_{2}}(Y,p_{2}\ ;A)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\operatorname{CH}^{c_{1}+c_{2}}(X\times Y,p_{1}+p_{2}\ ;A),\ [\Gamma_{1}]\otimes[\Gamma_{2}]\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces[\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}]

with the pairings constructed in Lemma
3.1
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
X
and
Y
be smooth and equi-dimensional algebraic schemes over a perfect field
k
. Let
n\geq 2
be an integer. Then the following diagram

(3.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p\ ;\mathbb{Z}/n)\otimes\operatorname{CH}^{j}(Y,2j-i\ ;\mathbb{Z}/n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{CH}^{j+c}(X\times Y,p+2j-i\ ;\mathbb{Z}/n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p\ ;\mathbb{Z}/n)\otimes H^{i-1}_{i-j-2,nr}(Y,(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{Y}(j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{2c-p+i-1}_{c-p+i-j-2,nr}(X\times Y,(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{X\times Y}(c+j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})}}
(
3.6
)
(
3.1
)
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}\otimes\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}

commutes, where we use the symbol
\theta
for the coboundary maps of (
2.4
).

Proof.

We first recall the description of the coboundary map

\theta:H^{i-1}_{i-j-2,nr}(Y,(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{X}(j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})\to\operatorname{CH}^{j}(Y,2j-i\ ;\mathbb{Z}/n)

from
[
KZ24
, Proposition 4.15, (27)]
. Note that although the construction of
\theta
in
[
KZ24
, Proposition 4.15]
is stated only for
n\in k^{\ast}
invertible, it can also be generalised to
n=q^{r}
with
q=\operatorname{char}(k)>0
; cf.
[
AS24
, Corollary 1.3]
. Let
[\alpha]\in H^{i-1}_{i-j-2,nr}(Y,(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{Y}(j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})
, where we can assume
\alpha\in H^{i-1}(Y\setminus R,(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{X}(j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})
for some equi-dimensional closed subscheme
R\subset Y
of codimension
\geq(i-j)
. The cycle class map
\operatorname{cl}_{R}:H^{i}_{R}(Y_{\text{Zar}},(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{Y}(j)_{\text{Zar}})\to H^{i}_{R}(Y_{\text{\'{e}t}},(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{Y}(j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})
can be then checked to be an isomorphism. Indeed, this is a formal consequence of the Beilinson–Lichtenbaum conjecture (see Theorem
2.2
), if one considers the relevant Zariski hypercohomology spectral sequences and note that
E^{p,q}_{2}=H^{p}_{R}(Y_{\text{Zar}},R^{q}\pi_{\ast}(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{Y}(j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})=0
for
p<i-j
by a simple argument using the Gersten resolution; see
[
BO74
,
GS88
]
. The class
\theta([\alpha])
is then given as the value of
\alpha
by the following map

(3.8)

\begin{split}&H^{i-1}(Y\setminus R,(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{X}(j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})\overset{\partial}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}H^{i}_{R}(Y_{\text{\'{e}t}},(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{Y}(j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})\overset{\operatorname{cl}_{R}^{-1}}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}H^{i}_{R}(Y_{\text{Zar}},(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{Y}(j)_{\text{Zar}})\\
&\overset{\iota_{\ast}}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}H^{i}(Y_{\text{Zar}},(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{Y}(j)_{\text{Zar}})=\operatorname{CH}^{j}(Y,2j-i\ ;\mathbb{Z}/n),\end{split}

where
\partial
is the coboundary map of the long exact étale cohomology sequence associated to the pair
(R,Y)
; see
[
Mil80
, III.1.25]
. In particular, we have
\theta(\alpha)=[\iota_{\ast}\operatorname{cl}^{-1}_{R}\partial(\alpha)]
.

Next, assume that
c>p
and let
[\Gamma]\in\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p\ ;\mathbb{Z}/n)
. Choose an equi-dimensional closed subscheme
Z\subset X
of codimension-
(c-p)
in
X
, such that
\operatorname{supp}(\Gamma)\subset Z\times\Delta^{p}
. Since
\theta
is compatible with flat pull-backs, we find

\eqref{eq:exterior-product-chow}\circ(\operatorname{id}\otimes\theta)([\Gamma]\otimes[\alpha])=[p^{\ast}\Gamma\cup\iota_{\ast}\operatorname{cl}^{-1}_{X\times R}\partial(q^{\ast}\alpha)]=\iota_{\ast}[p^{\ast}\Gamma\cup\operatorname{cl}^{-1}_{X\times R}\partial(q^{\ast}\alpha)],

where in the last equality, we view
\Gamma
as a class in
H^{2c-p}_{Z}(X_{\text{Zar}},(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{X}(c)_{\text{Zar}})
.

On the other hand, the constructive proof of Lemma
3.1
, shows that

\eqref{eq:pairing}([\Gamma]\otimes[\alpha])=[\iota_{\ast}\text{Exc}(p^{\ast}\operatorname{cl}_{Z}(\Gamma)\cup q^{\ast}\alpha)],

where the notation here is consistent with the one used in (
3.4
). We thus obtain

\theta\circ\eqref{eq:pairing}([\Gamma]\otimes[\alpha])=[\iota_{\ast}\operatorname{cl}^{-1}_{Z\times R}\partial(\text{Exc}(p^{\ast}\operatorname{cl}_{Z}(\Gamma)\cup q^{\ast}\alpha))].

We finally need to show the equality
\operatorname{cl}^{-1}_{Z\times R}\partial(\text{Exc}(p^{\ast}\operatorname{cl}_{Z}(\Gamma)\cup q^{\ast}\alpha))=p^{\ast}\Gamma\cup\operatorname{cl}^{-1}_{X\times R}\partial(q^{\ast}\alpha)
or equivalently
\partial(\text{Exc}(p^{\ast}\operatorname{cl}_{Z}(\Gamma)\cup q^{\ast}\alpha))=p^{\ast}\operatorname{cl}_{Z}(\Gamma)\cup\partial(q^{\ast}\alpha)
. The latter translates into a compatibility problem of cup products with residue maps, i.e., it suffices to know if the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{i-1}(X\times(Y\setminus R),(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{X\times Y}(j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{i}_{X\times R}(X\times Y,(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{X\times Y}(j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{i-1+2c-p}_{Z\times R}(X\times Y\setminus(Z\times R),(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{X\times Y}(j+c)_{\text{\'{e}t}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{i+2c-p}_{Z\times R}(X\times Y,(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{X\times Y}(j+c)_{\text{\'{e}t}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\text{Exc}\circ(\operatorname{cl}_{Z\times Y}(p^{\ast}\Gamma)\cup-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{cl}_{Z\times Y}(p^{\ast}\Gamma)\cup-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}

commutes. This in turn is well known; see
[
Sch24b
, Lemma B.2]
. This proves the commutativity of (
3.7
) in the case
c>p
.

The case
c\leq p
remains to be considered. In the above discussion, we can then take
Z=X
and observe that

\theta\circ\eqref{eq:pairing}([\Gamma]\otimes[\alpha])=[\iota_{\ast}\operatorname{cl}^{-1}_{X\times R}\partial(p^{\ast}\operatorname{cl}_{X}(\Gamma)\cup q^{\ast}\alpha)].

Thus, as before it is enough to note that
\partial(p^{\ast}\operatorname{cl}_{X}(\Gamma)\cup q^{\ast}\alpha)=p^{\ast}\operatorname{cl}_{X}(\Gamma)\cup\partial(q^{\ast}\alpha)
. The latter equality follows from
[
Sch21
, Lemma 2.4]
. The proof of the lemma is now complete.
∎

Corollary 3.4
.

Let
X
and
Y
be smooth and equi-dimensional algebraic schemes over a perfect field
k
. Let
n\geq 2
be an integer and consider the natural exterior product map

(3.9)

\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p\ ;\mathbb{Z}/n)\otimes F^{j,i}_{n}(Y)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces F^{c+j,2c-p+i}_{n}(X\times Y),\ [\Gamma_{1}]\otimes[\Gamma_{2}]\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces[\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}]

induced from (
3.6
), where recall that the cycle group
F^{j,i}_{n}(Y)
is defined in (
2.4
). Then (
3.9
) identifies through the isomorphisms (
2.38
) with the pairing

(3.10)

\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p\ ;\mathbb{Z}/n)\otimes\frac{H^{i-1}_{i-j-2,nr}(Y,(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{Y}(j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})}{H^{i-1}_{L}(Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(j))}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\frac{H^{2c-p+i-1}_{c-p+i-j-2,nr}(X\times Y,(\mathbb{Z}/n)_{X\times Y}(c+j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})}{H^{2c-p+i-1}_{L}(X\times Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(c+j))}

induced from (
3.1
).

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of Lemma
3.3
.∎

Remark 3.5
.

An argument in the same line as the one made in Lemma
3.3
shows that the pairing

\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p)\otimes\frac{H^{i-2}_{i-j-3,nr}(Y,(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})_{Y}(j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})}{H^{i-2}_{L}(Y,\mathbb{Z}(j))}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\frac{H^{2c-p+i-2}_{c-p+i-j-3,nr}(X\times Y,(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})_{X\times Y}(c+j)_{\text{\'{e}t}})}{H^{2c-p+i-2}_{L}(X\times Y,\mathbb{Z}(c+j))}

given by (
3.1
) identifies with the natural exterior product map

\operatorname{CH}^{c}(X,p)\otimes G^{j,i}(Y)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces G^{c+j,2c-p+i}(X\times Y),

where the cycle group
G^{j,i}
is defined in (
2.4
).

4.
Injectivity results

In this section we prove the main technical results of this paper. The following results and their proofs should be compared with
[
Sch24a
, Theorem 6.1]
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field and let
R=\mathcal{O}_{C,x_{0}}
be the local ring of a smooth
k
-curve
C
at a point
x_{0}\in C(k)
. Let
K
denote an algebraic closure of the fraction field
\operatorname{Frac}(R)
and let
n\geq 2
be an integer invertible in
k
. Assume that there is a projective flat
R
-family
\mathcal{X}\to\operatorname{Spec}R
with connected fibres of relative dimension
d
whose total space
\mathcal{X}
is integral and its generic fibre
X_{\eta}:=\mathcal{X}\times_{R}K
is smooth and such that the following properties are satisfied, where
X:=\mathcal{X}_{\bar{\eta}}
is the geometric generic fibre of the family:

(\textbf{P1})

There exist classes
\alpha_{j}\in H^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\'{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n),

j=1,2,\ldots,r,
such that the elements
\delta(\alpha_{j}|_{X})\in H^{2}_{\operatorname{cont}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{n})[n]
form generators, where
\delta:H^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n)\to H^{2}_{\operatorname{cont}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{n})
is the Bockstein map and such that

(\textbf{P2})

for each component
X_{0i}
of the central fibre
X_{0}=\mathcal{X}\times_{R}k
, the restriction
\alpha_{j}|_{X_{0i}}\in H^{1}(X_{0i},\mathbb{Z}/n)
is zero.

Then for any smooth projective variety
Y_{k}
over
k
with base change
Y:=Y_{k}\times_{k}K
and for any free
\mathbb{Z}/n
-submodule
M\subset\operatorname{CH}^{d}(X,1)/n,
the exterior product maps

\begin{split}&\Lambda_{1}:M\otimes\frac{H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y_{k},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))}{H^{i}(Y_{k},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces M\otimes\frac{H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(j))}{H^{i}(Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(j))}\overset{\eqref{eq:pairing}}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\frac{H^{2d+i-1}_{r+d-1,nr}(X\times Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(d+j))}{H^{2d+i-1}(X\times Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(d+j))}\\
&\Lambda_{2}:M\otimes H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y_{k},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces M\otimes H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(j))\overset{\eqref{eq:pairing}}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}H^{2d+i-1}_{r+d-1,nr}(X\times Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(d+j))\end{split}

are both injective for all integers
i,r\geq 0
.

Proof.

We first prove that
\Lambda_{1}
is injective. We can write
M=\bigoplus_{\nu=1}^{N}\mathbb{Z}/n\cdot z_{\nu}
, where the classes
z_{\nu}\in\operatorname{CH}^{d}(X,1)_{\operatorname{tors}}
can be chosen to be torsion; see Lemma
2.6
. Let
c:=\sum^{N}_{\nu=1}z_{\nu}\otimes[\beta_{k,\nu}]\in\ker(\Lambda_{1}),
where
\beta_{k,\nu}\in H^{i}(F_{r+1}Y_{k},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))
. We can then find an equi-dimensional closed subscheme
R_{k}\subset Y_{k}
of codimension-
(r+2)
in
Y_{k}
, such that
\beta_{k,\nu}\in H^{i}(Y_{k}\setminus R_{k},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))
for all
\nu=1,2,\ldots,N
. Moreover let
Z\subset X
be an equi-dimensional closed subscheme of
X
of codimension-
(d-1)
in
X
such that
\operatorname{supp}(z_{\nu})\subset Z\times\Delta^{1}
for all
\nu=1,2,\ldots,N
. We denote by
\beta_{\nu}
the image of
\beta_{k,\nu}
in
H^{i}(Y\setminus R,\mathbb{Z}/n(j)),
where
R:=R_{k}\times_{k}K
. Then
\Lambda_{1}(c)
is given as the class of the element

\gamma:=\sum^{N}_{\nu=1}\iota_{\ast}\operatorname{Exc}(p^{\ast}\operatorname{cl}_{Z}(z_{\nu})\cup q^{\ast}\beta_{\nu})\in H^{2d+i-1}(X\times Y\setminus(Z\times R),\mathbb{Z}/n(j+d));

see proof of Lemma
3.1
, where
p:X\times Y\to X
and
q:X\times Y\to Y
are the natural projections. Note that if we further restrict the class
\gamma
to
X\times U,
where
U:=Y\setminus R
(respectively, we keep the notation
U_{k}:=Y_{k}\setminus R_{k}
), then the class takes the form

\gamma|_{X\times U}:=\sum^{N}_{\nu=1}p^{\ast}\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu})\cup q^{\ast}\beta_{\nu}\in H^{2d+i-1}(X\times U,\mathbb{Z}/n(j+d)).

Now,

\Lambda_{1}(c)=0\iff[\gamma]\in\operatorname{im}(H^{2d+i-1}(X\times Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(j+d)))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{2d+i-1}_{r+d-1,nr}(X\times Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(j+d))).

The latter relation also implies that

(4.1)

[\gamma|_{X\times U}]\in\operatorname{im}(H^{2d+i-1}(X\times Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(j+d))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{2d+i-1}_{r+d-1,nr}(X\times U,\mathbb{Z}/n(j+d))).

The classical Gysin sequence
[
CTS21
,
\lx@sectionsign
2.3.2]
induces a long exact sequence

(4.2)

\begin{split}&\cdots\overset{\partial}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\bigoplus_{w\in(X\times U)^{(r+d)}}H^{i-1-2r}(F_{0}\overline{\{\omega\}},\mathbb{Z}/n(j-r))\overset{\iota_{\ast}}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}H^{2d+i-1}(F_{r+d}(X\times U),\mathbb{Z}/n(j+d))\\
&\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{2d+i-1}(F_{r+d-1}(X\times U),\mathbb{Z}/n(j+d))\overset{\partial}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\cdots;\end{split}

see
[
Sch23
, Lemma 5.8]
. Thus, condition (
4.1
) together with the exact sequence (
4.2
) show that there exists
\xi\in\bigoplus_{w\in(X\times U)^{(r+d)}}H^{i-1-2r}(F_{0}\overline{\{\omega\}},\mathbb{Z}/n(j-r)),
such that

(4.3)

\epsilon:=\iota_{\ast}\xi+\gamma|_{X\times U}\in\operatorname{im}(H^{2d+i-1}(X\times Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(j+d)))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{2d+i-1}(F_{r+d}(X\times U),\mathbb{Z}/n(j+d))).

The cycle class map
\operatorname{cl}_{X}:\operatorname{CH}^{d}(X,1)/n\hookrightarrow H^{2d-1}(X_{\text{\'{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n(d))
is injective; see Lemma
2.4
(
1
). Thus, the classes
\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu})
with
\nu=1,2,\ldots,N
generate a free
\mathbb{Z}/n
-submodule of
H^{2d-1}(X_{\text{\'{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n(d))
of rank
N
. We claim the following.

Claim 1
.

For any
\nu_{0}\in\{1,2,\ldots,N\}
, there exists a class
\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\in H^{1}(X_{\text{\'{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n)
with

(4.4)

\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu})=\begin{cases}\operatorname{cl}^{d}_{X}(pt),\ \text{if}\ \nu=\nu_{0}\\
0,\text{if}\ \nu\neq\nu_{0}.\end{cases}

Moreover, the property (
4.4
) still holds if we add to
\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}
a class from
H^{1}_{\operatorname{cont}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{n})
, i.e., it only depends on
\delta(\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})})\in H^{2}_{\operatorname{cont}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{n})[n]
.

Proof of Claim
1
.

The
Poincaré duality theorem for
X
yields a perfect pairing

H^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n)\otimes H^{2d-1}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n(d))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{2d}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n(d))=\mathbb{Z}/n\cdot\operatorname{cl}^{d}_{X}(pt);

see
[
Mil80
, Chapter VI, Corollary 11.2]
. In addition, the classes
\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})
are
\mathbb{Z}/n
linearly independent. This in turn implies the existence of
\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}
. For the last claim, recall that
z_{\nu}\in\operatorname{CH}^{d}(X,1)_{\operatorname{tors}}
is torsion. It follows that the cup product of the torsion class
\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu})\in H^{2d-1}_{\operatorname{cont}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{n}(d))
with any class of
H^{1}_{\operatorname{cont}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{n})
is zero, as
H^{2d}_{\operatorname{cont}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{n}(d))=\mathbb{Z}_{n}\cdot\operatorname{cl}_{X}(pt)
. Hence, the property (
4.4
) remains unchanged if we add to
\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}
a class from
H^{1}_{\operatorname{cont}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{n}),
as we wanted.
∎

We compute the class
p^{\ast}\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\epsilon
with the use of property (
4.4
) and then find from the relation (
4.3
) that

p^{\ast}\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\iota_{\ast}\xi+p^{\ast}\operatorname{cl}^{d}_{X}(pt)\cup q^{\ast}\beta_{\nu_{0}}\in\operatorname{im}(H^{2d+i}(X\times Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(j+d))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{2d+i}(F_{r+d}(X\times U),\mathbb{Z}/n(j+d))).

We further take pushforward of the above with respect to the proper map
q:X\times Y\to Y
and obtain that

(4.5)

q_{\ast}(p^{\ast}\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\iota_{\ast}\xi)+\beta_{\nu_{0}}\in\operatorname{im}(H^{i}(Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{i}(F_{r}U,\mathbb{Z}/n(j))).

Claim 2
.

We may assume that
\xi=\xi_{1}+\cdots+\xi_{\lambda},
where each
\xi_{t}\in H^{i-1-2r}(F_{0}\overline{\{\omega_{t}\}},\mathbb{Z}/n(j-r)),

t\in\{1,2,\ldots,\lambda\}
is supported at a point
\omega_{t}\in(X\times U)^{(r+d)}
with
q(\omega_{t})\in U^{(r)}
.

Proof of Claim
2
.

We may write by additivity that
q_{\ast}(p^{\ast}\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\iota_{\ast}\xi)=\sum_{t=1}^{\mu}q_{\ast}(p^{\ast}\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\iota_{t\ast}\xi_{t}),
where each
\xi_{t}\in H^{i-1-2r}(F_{0}\overline{\{\omega_{t}\}},\mathbb{Z}/n(j-r)),
with
\omega_{t}\in(X\times U)^{(r+d)}
and where
\iota_{t}:\overline{\{\omega_{t}\}}\hookrightarrow X\times U
is the natural embedding. It suffices to show that the term
q_{\ast}(p^{\ast}\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\iota_{t\ast}\xi_{t})
is zero if
\operatorname{codim}_{Y}(q(\omega_{t}))>r
. Indeed, the projection formula gives that
q_{\ast}(p^{\ast}\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\iota_{t\ast}\xi_{t})=(q\circ\iota_{t})_{\ast}((p\circ\iota_{t})^{\ast}\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\xi_{t})
. Thus, the condition
\operatorname{codim}_{Y}(q(\omega_{t}))>r,
in turn implies that

(q\circ\iota_{t})_{\ast}((p\circ\iota_{t})^{\ast}\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}|_{F_{0}\overline{\{p(\omega_{t})\}}}\cup\xi_{t})=0,

as we wanted.∎

Next, we show that the obstruction term
q_{\ast}(p^{\ast}\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\iota_{\ast}\xi)
vanishes after specialization. To this end, consider the constant family
\mathcal{U}:=U_{k}\times_{k}R\to\operatorname{Spec}R
and recall that we have a specialization map

\bar{sp}:H^{i}(F_{r}U,\mathbb{Z}/n(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces H^{i}(F_{r}U_{k},\mathbb{Z}/n(j));

see
\lx@sectionsign

2.6
and Proposition
2.13
(
5
). We want to prove the following claim.

Claim 3
.

We have that
\bar{sp}((q\circ\iota)_{\ast}((p\circ\iota)^{\ast}\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\xi))=0
.

Proof of Claim
3
.

First, by linearity and Claim
2
, we may assume that
\xi\in H^{i-1-2r}(F_{0}\overline{\{\omega\}},\mathbb{Z}/n(j-r))
for some
\omega\in(X\times U)^{(r+d)}
with
q(\omega)\in U^{(r)}
. We set
W:=\overline{\{\omega\}}\subset X\times U
and pick a finite field extension
F/\operatorname{Frac}(R)
over which
W
is defined, i.e.,
W=W_{F}\times_{F}K
with
W_{F}\subset X_{\eta}\times_{\operatorname{Frac}(R)}\mathcal{U}_{\eta}\times_{\operatorname{Frac}(R)}F
. Up to enlarging
F,
if necessary, we may also assume that
\xi
has a lift
\xi_{F}\in H^{i-1-2r}(F_{0}W_{F},\mathbb{Z}/n(j-r))
; see
[
Mil80
, Chapter III, Lemma 1.16, Remark 1.17 (a)]
. Letting
\tilde{R}
be a local ring at a maximal ideal of the normalization of
R
in
F
and performing the finite ramified base change
\tilde{R}/R,
we see that the properties

(
P1
)

and

(
P2
)

still remain true for the family
\mathcal{X}_{\tilde{R}}\to\operatorname{Spec}\tilde{R}
. This is clear as both maps
H^{1}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}/n)\to H^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n)
and
H^{1}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}/n)\to H^{1}(X_{0i},\mathbb{Z}/n)
factor through the pullback map
H^{1}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}/n)\to H^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{\tilde{R}},\mathbb{Z}/n)
. In particular, the above discussion tells us that we can take
F
as
\operatorname{Frac}(R),
i.e.,
W
is defined over the generic point of
R
and that
\xi
has a lift

\xi_{\operatorname{Frac}(R)}\in H^{i-1-2r}(F_{0}W_{\operatorname{Frac}(R)},\mathbb{Z}/n(j-r)).

Next, let
\mathcal{W}\subset\mathcal{X}\times_{R}\mathcal{U}\to\operatorname{Spec}R
be the schematic closure of
W_{\operatorname{Frac}(R)}\subset X_{\eta}\times_{\operatorname{Frac}(R)}U_{\eta}
inside
\mathcal{X}\times_{R}\mathcal{U}
and note that this is a flat
R
-family. It follows from
[
AK10
, Theorem 2.5, Corollary 2.2]
that after a finite ramified base change of discrete valuation rings
R^{\prime}/R
followed by normalizing the total space, we can achieve the result that the central fiber of the normalization
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}_{R^{\prime}}}\to\operatorname{Spec}R^{\prime}
is reduced. Thus, up to replacing
R^{\prime}
with
R,
we can assume that the central fibre of the normalization
f:\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}\to\operatorname{Spec}R
of
\mathcal{W}
is reduced.

The singular locus
\operatorname{Sing}(f)\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
of
f
in turn is a closed subscheme of codimension at least
2
in
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
. Therefore, if we set
\mathcal{S}:=q(\operatorname{Sing}(f))\subset\mathcal{U}
, then for the family
\mathcal{U}\setminus\mathcal{S}\to\operatorname{Spec}R
one has
F_{r}(\mathcal{U}_{\eta}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{\eta})=F_{r}(\mathcal{U}_{\eta})
along with
F_{r}(\mathcal{U}_{0}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{0})=F_{r}(\mathcal{U}_{0})
. Especially, we get a proper
R
-morphism
\tilde{q}:\mathcal{W}^{\prime}:=\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}\setminus q^{-1}(\mathcal{S})\to\mathcal{U}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{U}\setminus\mathcal{S},
whose both domain and target are given by smooth
R
-families with equi-dimensional fibres. We thus obtain a commutative diagram of the form

(4.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{i-2r}(F_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\eta},\mathbb{Z}/n(j-r))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{i-2r}(F_{0}\mathcal{W}^{\prime}_{0},\mathbb{Z}/n(j-r))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{i}(F_{r}\mathcal{U}_{\eta},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{H^{i}(F_{r}U_{k},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{q}_{\eta})_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{q}_{0})_{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces sp}

where
\tilde{q}_{\eta}
and
\tilde{q}_{0}
are the base changes of
\tilde{q}
over the generic and central fibre, respectively; see Proposition
2.13
(
4
). Note that here we drop the symbol
\pi
from the notation of the specialization maps due to Proposition
2.13
(
1
); see Remark
2.10
. We also denote by
\tilde{p}:\mathcal{W}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
the obvious map. We may assume that up to adding a class from
H^{1}_{\operatorname{cont}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{n}),
if necessary, the class
\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\in H^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n)
has a lift
\alpha_{\nu_{0}}\in H^{1}(\mathcal{X},\mu_{n})
that satisfies the property

(
P2
)

; see Claim
1
. Then by construction of
\bar{sp},
we have the equality

\begin{split}\bar{sp}((q\circ\iota)_{\ast}((p\circ\iota)^{\ast}\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\xi))&=sp((\tilde{q}_{\eta})_{\ast}(((\tilde{p}_{\eta})^{\ast}\alpha_{\nu_{0}})|_{F_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\eta}}\cup\xi_{\operatorname{Frac}(R)}))\\
&=(\tilde{q}_{0})_{\ast}(sp(((\tilde{p}_{\eta})^{\ast}\alpha_{\nu_{0}})|_{F_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\eta}}\cup\xi_{\operatorname{Frac}(R)}))\in H^{i}(F_{r}U_{k},\mathbb{Z}/n(j)).\end{split}

By Proposition
2.13
(
3
), we also find that
sp(((\tilde{p}_{\eta})^{\ast}\alpha_{\nu_{0}})|_{F_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\eta}}\cup\xi_{\operatorname{Frac}(R)})=\tilde{p}^{\ast}\alpha_{\nu_{0}}|_{F_{0}\mathcal{W}_{0}^{\prime}}\cup sp(\xi_{\operatorname{Frac}(R)})=0,
where the last term is zero, since
\tilde{p}^{\ast}\alpha_{\nu_{0}}|_{F_{0}\mathcal{W}_{0}^{\prime}}=0
by

(
P2
)

. This finishes the proof of the claim.
∎

Finally, note that
\bar{sp}(\beta_{\nu_{0}})=\beta_{k,\nu_{0}}
; see Proposition
2.13
(
3
). Thus, by (
4.5
), we see that Claim
3
together with Proposition
2.13
(
2
) imply that
\beta_{k,\nu_{0}}\in\operatorname{im}(H^{i}(Y_{k},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))\to H^{i}(F_{r}Y_{k},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))),
where we use that
F_{r}U_{k}=F_{r}Y_{k}
. This proves the injectivity of
\Lambda_{1}
.

We now explain how one can deduce the injectivity of
\Lambda_{2}
. With the same notation as above, we see that
\Lambda_{2}(c)=0
implies
[\gamma|_{X\times U}]=0\in H^{2d+i-1}_{r+d-1,nr}(X\times U,\mathbb{Z}/n(j+d))
. Thus, condition (
4.3
) becomes
\gamma|_{X\times U}+\iota_{\ast}\xi=0\in H^{2d+i-1}(F_{r+d}(X\times U),\mathbb{Z}/n(j+d))
and instead of (
4.5
), one in turn gets
q_{\ast}(p^{\ast}\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\iota_{\ast}\xi)+\beta_{\nu_{0}}=0\in H^{i}(F_{r}U,\mathbb{Z}/n(j))
. Hence, as before Claim
3
along with Proposition
2.13
(
3
) yield that

0=\bar{sp}(q_{\ast}(p^{\ast}\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\iota_{\ast}\xi)+\beta_{\nu_{0}})=\bar{sp}(\beta_{\nu_{0}})=\beta_{k,\nu_{0}}\in H^{i}(F_{r}Y_{k},\mathbb{Z}/n(j)),

as we wanted. This proves the injectivity of
\Lambda_{2}
. The proof is complete.
∎

Remark 4.2
.

If one fixes the free
\mathbb{Z}/n
-submodule
M=\bigoplus_{\nu=1}^{N}\mathbb{Z}/n\cdot z_{\nu}\subset\operatorname{CH}^{d}(X,1)/n
in Theorem
4.1
, then both properties

(
P1
)

and

(
P2
)

can be relaxed further. In fact, for

(
P1
)

, we only need that

\delta(\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu})})\in\operatorname{im}(H^{1}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}/n)\to H^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n)\overset{\delta}{\to}H^{2}_{\operatorname{cont}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{n})),

where
\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu})}\in H^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n)
is a Poincaré dual of the class
\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu})\in H^{2d-1}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n(d))
; see Claim
1
. In addition, for

(
P2
)

, it suffices to require that the lifts of
\delta(\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu})})
in
H^{1}(\mathcal{X},\mathbb{Z}/n)
can be chosen in such a way that they become zero when restricted to the components
X_{0i}
of the central fibre
X_{0}:=\mathcal{X}\times_{R}k
.

Theorem 4.3
.

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field and let
R=\mathcal{O}_{C,x_{0}}
be the local ring of a smooth
k
-curve
C
at a point
x_{0}\in C(k)
. Let
K
denote an algebraic closure of the fraction field
\operatorname{Frac}(R)
and let
n\geq 2
be an integer invertible in
k
. Assume that there is a projective flat
R
-family
\mathcal{X}\to\operatorname{Spec}R
with connected fibres of relative dimension
d
whose total space
\mathcal{X}
is integral and its generic fibre
X_{\eta}:=\mathcal{X}\times_{R}K
is smooth and such that the following properties are satisfied, where
X:=\mathcal{X}_{\bar{\eta}}
is the geometric generic fibre of the family:

(\textbf{P'1})

There exist classes
\alpha_{j}\in H^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{\text{\'{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n),

j=1,2,\ldots,r,
such that the elements
\alpha_{j}|_{X}\in H^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n)
form generators and such that

(\textbf{P'2})

for each component
X_{0i}
of the central fibre
X_{0}=\mathcal{X}\times_{R}k
, the restriction
\alpha_{j}|_{X_{0i}}\in H^{1}(X_{0i},\mathbb{Z}/n)
is zero.

Then for any smooth projective variety
Y_{k}
over
k
with base change
Y:=Y_{k}\times_{k}K
and any free
\mathbb{Z}/n
-submodule
M\subset\operatorname{CH}^{d}(X,1\ ;\mathbb{Z}/n),
the exterior product maps

\begin{split}&\Lambda_{1}:M\otimes\frac{H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y_{k},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))}{H^{i}(Y_{k},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces M\otimes\frac{H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(j))}{H^{i}(Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(j))}\overset{\eqref{eq:pairing}}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}\frac{H^{2d+i-1}_{r+d-1,nr}(X\times Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(d+j))}{H^{2d+i-1}(X\times Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(d+j))}\\
&\Lambda_{2}:M\otimes H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y_{k},\mathbb{Z}/n(j))\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces M\otimes H^{i}_{r,nr}(Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(j))\overset{\eqref{eq:pairing}}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}H^{2d+i-1}_{r+d-1,nr}(X\times Y,\mathbb{Z}/n(d+j))\end{split}

are both injective for all integers
i,r\geq 0
.

Proof.

Recall that we have a canonical isomorphism
\operatorname{cl}_{X}:\operatorname{CH}^{d}(X,1\ ;\mathbb{Z}/n)\simeq H^{2d-1}(X_{\text{\'{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n(d));
see Lemma
2.4
(
1
). Hence, if
M:=\bigoplus^{N}_{\nu=1}\mathbb{Z}/n\cdot z_{\nu}
, then
\operatorname{cl}_{X}(M)\subset H^{2d-1}(X_{\text{\'{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n(d))
is a free
\mathbb{Z}/n
-submodule of rank
N
generated by the classes
\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu})
with
\nu=1,2,\ldots,N
.

Let us explain why we need the slightly stronger condition

(
P’1
)

here. This is related to Claim
1
. Namely, the cup product
\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\cup\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu}),
where
\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}\in H^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n)
is a Poincaré dual of the class
\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})\in H^{2d-1}(X_{\text{\'{e}t}},\mathbb{Z}/n(d)),
(see (
4.4
)) might change if we add to
\overline{\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})}
a class that lies in the image of
H^{1}_{\operatorname{cont}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{n})\to H^{1}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n)
. Indeed, if
z_{\nu_{0}}\notin\operatorname{CH}^{d}(X,1)/n,
then the class
\operatorname{cl}_{X}(z_{\nu_{0}})\in H^{2d-1}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n(d))
no longer admits a lift in
H^{2d-1}_{\operatorname{cont}}(X,\mathbb{Z}_{n}(d))_{\operatorname{tors}}
; see Lemma
2.6
. The latter can of course occur if and only if the first Betti number
b_{1}

[
CTS21
, Remark 5.2.7]
of
X
is non-zero by Roitman’s theorem
[
Blo79
,
Roj80
]
and the following exact sequence

0\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\operatorname{CH}^{d}(X,1)/n\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 14.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
